# Product Ad Storyboard Style Pack

## Use When

- 产品广告、TVC、投流视频、卖点片、转化广告。
- 产品需要早出现，并通过使用动作、材质、结果变化建立记忆。
- 香水宣传片如果目标是突出单品、气味卖点、瓶身记忆、购买转化或 packshot，优先使用本 pack。

## Do Not Use When

- 主要讲品牌价值观，产品只是氛围道具。
- 主要是招聘、招商、融资或发布会功能演示。
- brief 明确要求平台原生用户种草口吻。

## Visual Tone

产品中心、清楚、直接、有记忆点。

## Palette

根据产品定位决定，可高对比；背景不能抢产品。

## Lighting

产品 key light 明确；产品细节有可见高光和材质。

## Camera Language

产品特写、snap zoom、lateral dolly、tracking、before/after 对比镜头。

## Editing Rhythm

快，信息密度高，痛点到产品到证明到 CTA。

## Subtitle Placement

规格/卖点作为 super text，短词，最多三词一组。

## Actor Behavior

人在真实使用产品，不对镜头表演喜欢。

## Product Treatment

产品必须是主角。特写、使用动作、结果变化都要明确。

## Forbidden Motifs

只有 lifestyle 没产品、抽象概念镜头、产品过晚出现、多个卖点抢主线。

# Storyboard Reviewer

## 1. 使用场景

当 `storyboard/storyboard_v{N}.md`、`storyboard/storyboard_v{N}.json`、`storyboard/storyboard_v{N}.csv` 生成后，制片人 Agent 必须使用本审核规范执行自动审核。

输出：

```text
storyboard/review_v{N}.md
storyboard/review_v{N}.json
```

`review_v{N}.json` 必须符合：

```text
video-agent/schemas/review.schema.json
```

---

## 2. 输入

必须检查：

```text
{session_root}/brief/normalized_brief.json
{session_root}/script/script_v{N}.json
{session_root}/storyboard/storyboard_v{N}.md
{session_root}/storyboard/storyboard_v{N}.json
{session_root}/storyboard/storyboard_v{N}.csv
video-agent/schemas/storyboard.schema.json
```

---

## 3. Blocking checks

以下任一失败，verdict 必须为 `needs_revision` 或 `fail`：

1. Markdown、JSON 或 CSV 文件不存在。
2. `storyboard_v{N}.json` 不是合法 JSON。
3. `storyboard_v{N}.json` 不符合 `storyboard.schema.json`。
4. `shots` 为空。
5. shot_id 不唯一。
6. shot 引用了 script 中不存在的 scene_id。
7. shot 引用了 script 中不存在的 beat_id。
8. 每个 shot 缺少 duration_sec、shot_size、camera_angle、camera_movement、visual、sound、transition、aspect_ratio 任一核心字段。
9. 总时长偏离 target_duration_sec 超过 duration_tolerance_sec。
10. 任一 shot 缺少 asset_requirements。
11. CSV 与 JSON 明显不一致。

---

## 4. Non-blocking checks

以下问题可作为 non_blocking_issues 或 suggestions：

1. 某些 shot 时长过长，超过 8 秒。
2. 镜头语言重复，景别或运镜变化少。
3. generation_prompt 不够具体。
4. negative_prompt 太弱。
5. 音效层次不够丰富。
6. 转场策略单一但不影响 MVP。

---

## 5. Scoring

建议分数：

- 1.0：结构完整、镜头清晰、总时长合理、资产需求完整。
- 0.8-0.9：小问题，不阻塞进入人工审核。
- 0.6-0.79：有明显问题，需要修改。
- <0.6：结构或覆盖严重不合格。

只有当：

```text
verdict = pass
blocking_issues = []
```

才允许制片人 Agent 请求人工审核。

---

## 6. 输出要求

review JSON 必须符合统一结构，stage 必须是：

```text
storyboard
```

review Markdown 应摘要说明：

- 审核结论
- 分数
- blocking issues
- non-blocking issues
- shot 覆盖情况
- 总时长检查
- 是否允许进入人工审核

```

name: asset-planner
description: Use when Video Agent has approved script and storyboard outputs and must extract visual assets, generate asset reference images, then generate cinematic storyboard frame images under the session assets directory while keeping assets_manifest_v{N}.json valid against assets-manifest.schema.json.
version: 1.0.0
author: Hermes Video Agent
license: MIT
metadata:
  hermes:
    tags: [video-agent, asset-planning, image-generation, storyboard-frames, manifest, schema-contract]
    related_skills: [video-producer, mock-scriptwriter, mock-storyboard, doubao-seedance-shot-generator]
---

# Asset Planner Skill

## 1. Overview

`asset-planner` is the Video Agent asset-stage skill. It sits after the upstream scriptwriter and storyboard skills:

```text
scriptwriter -> storyboard -> asset-planner -> asset-reviewer -> shot/video generation
```

Its job is not only to make a mock manifest. It must use the approved script and approved storyboard together to:

1. Extract reusable production assets: characters, character turnarounds, scenes/backgrounds, props, UI/FX cues, style references, visual systems, and shot-critical image needs.
2. Write a strict schema-valid asset manifest:
   ```text
   {session_root}/assets/assets_manifest_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
   {session_root}/assets/assets_manifest_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.md
   ```
3. Optionally generate actual reusable asset reference images and save them inside the current session assets folder.
4. Generate a complete set of cinematic storyboard frame images for every storyboard shot, using the script, storyboard, and generated asset references as continuity anchors.
5. Keep all asset-stage outputs inside:
   ```text
   {session_root}/assets/
   ```

The machine-readable asset manifest must validate against:

```text
video-agent/schemas/assets-manifest.schema.json
```

Do not use the old standalone image-storyboard project layout (`project_manifest.json`, `storyboard-images/`, `managed-photos/`, `deliverables/`) inside Video Agent sessions. In Video Agent, asset reference images and storyboard frame images both live under the session `assets/` tree.

## 2. When to Use

Use this skill when:

- The script stage and storyboard stage have both been approved.
- The user asks to extract assets from the script and storyboard.
- The user asks for character, scene, prop, visual-style, UI/FX, or frame reference images before video generation.
- The user asks to generate a full set of realistic cinematic storyboard images from the approved script/storyboard.
- A downstream shot/video generator needs `assets/assets_manifest_v{N}.json` plus local image references for consistent image-to-video or multiframe workflows.
- Script or storyboard changes make previous assets stale and the assets stage must be regenerated.

Do not use this skill for:

- Writing the script itself.
- Writing the storyboard table itself.
- Submitting video-generation jobs.
- Creating final edited videos.
- Adding non-schema fields into `assets_manifest_v{N}.json`.

## 3. Required Inputs

The producer or caller must provide a session root under:

```text
video-agent/video-generation-session/{topic_name}/{session_id}/
```

Required files:

```text
{session_root}/production_state.json
{session_root}/script/script_v{SCRIPT_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/script/script_v{SCRIPT_VERSION}.md
{session_root}/storyboard/storyboard_v{STORYBOARD_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/storyboard/storyboard_v{STORYBOARD_VERSION}.md
video-agent/schemas/script.schema.json
video-agent/schemas/storyboard.schema.json
video-agent/schemas/assets-manifest.schema.json
```

Before generating anything, verify:

- `production_state.json` exists and parses.
- `script` stage is `approved` and not `stale`.
- `storyboard` stage is `approved` and not `stale`.
- `script_v{N}.json` validates against `script.schema.json`.
- `storyboard_v{N}.json` validates against `storyboard.schema.json`.
- The script/storyboard versions match `production_state.json`.

If any prerequisite fails, stop and report the blocker. Do not create assets from stale or unapproved upstream files.

## 4. Output Contract and Paths

All outputs must stay under:

```text
{session_root}/assets/
```

### 4.1 Canonical schema outputs

Required for every asset-planner run:

```text
{session_root}/assets/assets_manifest_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/assets/assets_manifest_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.md
```

Initial version is `v001`; regeneration increments to `v002`, `v003`, etc.

### 4.2 Generated reusable asset image paths

The current `assets-manifest.schema.json` has `additionalProperties: false`. It does not define image path fields. Therefore, never add `image_path`, `local_path`, `url`, `file`, `generated_image`, or `reference_image` to `assets_manifest_v{N}.json`.

Actual reusable asset images must be copied into:

```text
{session_root}/assets/generated/{ASSETS_VERSION}/{asset_id}_{asset_key}.png
```

Examples:

```text
assets/generated/v001/asset_001_char_001_protagonist_turnaround.png
assets/generated/v001/asset_006_scene_001_rainy_alley_background.png
assets/generated/v001/asset_018_prop_001_phone_notification.png
```

Required operational sidecars for production image mode:

```text
{session_root}/assets/generated/assets_generation_queue_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/assets/generated/assets_generation_status_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
```

Optional convenience output:

```text
{session_root}/assets/generated/contact_sheet_assets_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.png
```

These sidecars are the canonical machine-readable place for local image paths because the schema JSON may not contain them. They must use session-relative paths so every other skill can resolve them by joining with `{session_root}`.

`assets_generation_queue_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json` must map every manifest asset to its target file before image generation:

```json
{
  "session_id": "topic_no1_yyyymmdd",
  "version": "v001",
  "items": [
    {
      "asset_id": "asset_001",
      "asset_key": "char_001_turnaround",
      "asset_type": "character_turnaround",
      "priority": "required",
      "target_path": "assets/generated/v001/asset_001_char_001_turnaround.png",
      "prompt": "...",
      "negative_prompt": "...",
      "status": "queued"
    }
  ]
}
```

`assets_generation_status_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json` must be updated after file verification and must be indexable by both `asset_id` and `asset_key`:

```json
{
  "session_id": "topic_no1_yyyymmdd",
  "version": "v001",
  "generated": [
    {
      "asset_id": "asset_001",
      "asset_key": "char_001_turnaround",
      "path": "assets/generated/v001/asset_001_char_001_turnaround.png",
      "size": [1024, 1536],
      "bytes": 123456,
      "qc_status": "pending_manual_qc"
    }
  ],
  "failed": [],
  "pending": []
}
```

If an image is not generated yet, leave the manifest asset `status` as `planned` and list it under `pending`; do not invent a file path as if it exists.

### 4.3 Generated full storyboard frame image paths

After reusable assets are planned or generated, generate the full storyboard image set under the same `assets/` stage:

```text
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/{ASSETS_VERSION}/{shot_id}_A.png
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/{ASSETS_VERSION}/{shot_id}_B.png
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/{ASSETS_VERSION}/{shot_id}_C.png
```

Use named variants intentionally:

```text
A = hero keyframe / most representative shot beat
B = setup, before-state, insert, or asset-context frame
C = result, after-state, reaction, or continuity-completion frame
D = optional extra detail frame when the shot cannot read in one still
```

Required storyboard-frame sidecar files when frame generation is planned or run:

```text
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frame_queue_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frame_status_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frames_manifest_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frames_manifest_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.md
```

Optional convenience output:

```text
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/contact_sheet_storyboard_frames_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.png
```

These storyboard-frame sidecars are operational outputs, not replacements for `assets_manifest_v{N}.json` and not governed by `assets-manifest.schema.json` unless a dedicated storyboard-frame schema is added later. They are the canonical place where downstream shot generators should look for complete still-frame references.

`storyboard_frame_queue_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json` must list planned frames in shot order, variant order, and session-relative target paths:

```json
{
  "session_id": "topic_no1_yyyymmdd",
  "version": "v001",
  "source_storyboard_version": "v001",
  "source_assets_version": "v001",
  "frames": [
    {
      "shot_id": "shot_001",
      "variant": "A",
      "frame_id": "shot_001_A",
      "target_path": "assets/storyboard_frames/v001/shot_001_A.png",
      "asset_keys": ["char_001_turnaround", "scene_001_background"],
      "asset_image_paths": ["assets/generated/v001/asset_001_char_001_turnaround.png"],
      "prompt": "...",
      "negative_prompt": "...",
      "status": "queued"
    }
  ]
}
```

`storyboard_frame_status_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json` must record generation/QC status with the same `frame_id`, `shot_id`, `variant`, and `path` fields:

```json
{
  "session_id": "topic_no1_yyyymmdd",
  "version": "v001",
  "frames": [
    {
      "frame_id": "shot_001_A",
      "shot_id": "shot_001",
      "variant": "A",
      "path": "assets/storyboard_frames/v001/shot_001_A.png",
      "size": [1024, 1536],
      "bytes": 123456,
      "status": "generated",
      "qc_status": "pending_manual_qc"
    }
  ],
  "missing_shot_ids": [],
  "failed": []
}
```

`storyboard_frames_manifest_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json` is the stable downstream index. It must group frames by shot so a video shot generator can resolve reference stills without scraping Markdown:

```json
{
  "session_id": "topic_no1_yyyymmdd",
  "version": "v001",
  "source_storyboard_version": "v001",
  "source_assets_version": "v001",
  "shots": [
    {
      "shot_id": "shot_001",
      "frames": [
        {"variant": "A", "frame_id": "shot_001_A", "path": "assets/storyboard_frames/v001/shot_001_A.png", "status": "generated", "qc_status": "pending_manual_qc"}
      ]
    }
  ]
}
```

## 5. Asset Manifest Schema Rules

The JSON root must contain exactly the fields required by `assets-manifest.schema.json`:

```json
{
  "schema_version": "0.1.0",
  "session_id": "topic_no1_yyyymmdd",
  "version": "v001",
  "source_script_version": "v001",
  "source_storyboard_version": "v001",
  "title": "...",
  "asset_style_guide": {},
  "characters": [],
  "scenes": [],
  "props": [],
  "style_references": [],
  "shot_asset_map": [],
  "coverage": {},
  "generation_notes": {}
}
```

`generation_notes.skill` for new assets-stage manifests must be:

```json
"asset-planner"
```

### 5.1 Asset object fields

Every asset object in `characters`, `scenes`, `props`, and `style_references` must contain exactly:

```json
{
  "asset_id": "asset_001",
  "asset_key": "char_001_turnaround",
  "asset_type": "character_turnaround",
  "name": "主角角色三视图",
  "description": "稳定外观、服装、关键识别点和用途。",
  "source_ids": ["char_001", "shot_001"],
  "required_by_shots": ["shot_001"],
  "priority": "required",
  "generation_prompt": "Production-ready image prompt...",
  "negative_prompt": "low quality, blurry, watermark...",
  "deliverable": "turnaround",
  "status": "planned"
}
```

Allowed values:

- `asset_type`: `character`, `character_turnaround`, `scene`, `background`, `prop`, `style_reference`, `other`
- `priority`: `required`, `recommended`, `optional`
- `deliverable`: `image`, `turnaround`, `reference_board`, `none`
- `status`: `planned`, `generated`, `approved`, `rejected`, `stale`

`generated` means a file exists and opens. `approved` requires reviewer or human approval.

### 5.2 Asset arrays

Use these buckets:

- `characters`: characters and character turnarounds from script characters plus recurring storyboard roles.
- `scenes`: scenes/backgrounds from script scenes plus shot-specific locations.
- `props`: props, UI cues, screens, products, weapons, symbolic objects, physical metaphors, abstract FX, and shot-critical visual devices.

# Asset Reviewer

## 1. 使用场景

当 `assets/assets_manifest_v{N}.md` 和 `assets/assets_manifest_v{N}.json` 生成后，制片人 Agent 必须使用本审核规范执行自动审核。

输出：

```text
assets/review_v{N}.md
assets/review_v{N}.json
```

`review_v{N}.json` 必须符合：

```text
video-agent/schemas/review.schema.json
```

---

## 2. 输入

必须检查：

```text
{session_root}/script/script_v{N}.json
{session_root}/storyboard/storyboard_v{N}.json

{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frames_manifest_v{N}.json # storyboard-frame mode
video-agent/schemas/assets-manifest.schema.json
```

---

## 3. Blocking checks

以下任一失败，verdict 必须为 `needs_revision` 或 `fail`：

1. Markdown 或 JSON 文件不存在。
2. `assets_manifest_v{N}.json` 不是合法 JSON。
3. `assets_manifest_v{N}.json` 不符合 `assets-manifest.schema.json`。
4. script.characters 中的主要角色没有资产覆盖。
5. script.scenes 中的主要场景没有资产覆盖。
6. storyboard.shots 中某个 shot 没有出现在 shot_asset_map。
7. storyboard.shots.asset_requirements 中某个 required 需求没有被资产覆盖。
8. coverage.unresolved_asset_requirements 非空。
9. 任一 asset 缺少 asset_id、asset_key、asset_type、description、required_by_shots、generation_prompt。
10. 同一个 asset_key 生成多个重复资产且没有合并 required_by_shots。
11. 如果 manifest 中任一 asset.status 为 `generated` 或 `approved`，则必须能在 `assets/generated/assets_generation_status_v{N}.json.generated[]` 通过相同 `asset_id` 和 `asset_key` 找到 session-relative `path`，且该文件存在、非空、可打开。
12. 如果已生成 `assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frames_manifest_v{N}.json`，则每个 `storyboard.shots[*].shot_id` 必须在该 manifest 中出现；其中标记为 `generated` 的 frame path 必须存在、非空、可打开。
13. `assets_manifest_v{N}.json` 中不得出现 `image_path`、`local_path`、`url`、`file`、`generated_image`、`reference_image` 等 schema 未允许字段；图片路径只能在 Markdown 与 sidecar JSON 中。

---

## 4. Non-blocking checks

以下问题可作为 non_blocking_issues 或 suggestions：

1. 资产 prompt 不够具体。
2. negative_prompt 太弱。
3. style_references 为空。
4. 某些 recommended/optional 资产未覆盖。
5. 角色三视图描述不够稳定。
6. 场景资产缺少光照、色彩或构图说明。

---

## 5. Scoring

建议分数：

- 1.0：角色、场景、镜头需求全部覆盖，prompt 可执行。
- 0.8-0.9：小问题，不阻塞进入人工审核。
- 0.6-0.79：有明显覆盖或质量问题，需要修改。
- <0.6：资产规划不可用。

只有当：

```text
verdict = pass
blocking_issues = []
```

才允许制片人 Agent 请求人工审核。

---

## 6. 输出要求

review JSON 必须符合统一结构，stage 必须是：

```text
assets
```

review Markdown 应摘要说明：

- 审核结论
- 分数
- blocking issues
- non-blocking issues
- 角色覆盖
- 场景覆盖
- shot_asset_map 覆盖
- generated asset image sidecar path coverage (`assets/generated/assets_generation_status_v{N}.json`)
- storyboard frame sidecar path coverage (`assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frames_manifest_v{N}.json`) when frames were generated
- unresolved_asset_requirements
- 是否允许进入人工审核

name: "imagegen"
description: "Generate or edit raster images when the task benefits from AI-created bitmap visuals such as photos, illustrations, textures, sprites, mockups, or transparent-background cutouts. Use when Codex should create a brand-new image, transform an existing image, or derive visual variants from references, and the output should be a bitmap asset rather than repo-native code or vector. Do not use when the task is better handled by editing existing SVG/vector/code-native assets, extending an established icon or logo system, or building the visual directly in HTML/CSS/canvas."

# Image Generation Skill

Generates or edits images for the current project (for example website assets, game assets, UI mockups, product mockups, wireframes, logo design, photorealistic images, or infographics).

## Top-level modes and rules

This skill has exactly two top-level modes:

- **Default built-in tool mode (preferred):** built-in `image_gen` tool for normal image generation, editing, and simple transparent-image requests. Does not require `OPENAI_API_KEY`.
- **Fallback CLI mode:** `scripts/image_gen.py` CLI. Use when the user explicitly asks for the CLI/API/model path, or after the user explicitly confirms a true model-native transparency fallback with `gpt-image-1.5`. Requires `OPENAI_API_KEY`.

Within CLI fallback, the CLI exposes three subcommands:

- `generate`
- `edit`
- `generate-batch`

Rules:
- Use the built-in `image_gen` tool by default for normal image generation and editing requests.
- Do not switch to CLI fallback for ordinary quality, size, or file-path control.
- If the user explicitly asks for a transparent image/background, stay on built-in `image_gen` first: prompt for a flat removable chroma-key background, then remove it locally with the installed helper at `$CODEX_HOME/skills/.system/imagegen/scripts/remove_chroma_key.py`.
- Never silently switch from built-in `image_gen` or CLI `gpt-image-2` to CLI `gpt-image-1.5`. Treat this as a model/path downgrade and ask the user before doing it, unless the user has already explicitly requested `gpt-image-1.5`, `scripts/image_gen.py`, or CLI fallback.
- If a transparent request appears too complex for clean chroma-key removal, asks for true/native transparency, or local removal fails validation, explain that true transparency requires CLI `gpt-image-1.5 --background transparent --output-format png` because `gpt-image-2` does not support `background=transparent`, then ask whether to proceed. Run the CLI fallback only after the user confirms.
- The word `batch` by itself does not mean CLI fallback. If the user asks for many assets or says to batch-generate assets without explicitly asking for CLI/API/model controls, stay on the built-in path and issue one built-in call per requested asset or variant.
- If the built-in tool fails or is unavailable, tell the user the CLI fallback exists and that it requires `OPENAI_API_KEY`. Proceed only if the user explicitly asks for that fallback.
- If the user explicitly asks for CLI mode, use the bundled `scripts/image_gen.py` workflow. Do not create one-off SDK runners.
- Never modify `scripts/image_gen.py`. If something is missing, ask the user before doing anything else.

Built-in save-path policy:
- In built-in tool mode, Codex saves generated images under `$CODEX_HOME/*` by default.
- Do not describe or rely on OS temp as the default built-in destination.
- Do not describe or rely on a destination-path argument (if any) on the built-in `image_gen` tool. If a specific location is needed, generate first and then move or copy the selected output from `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...`.
- Save-path precedence in built-in mode:
  1. If the user names a destination, move or copy the selected output there.
  2. If the image is meant for the current project, move or copy the final selected image into the workspace before finishing.
  3. If the image is only for preview or brainstorming, render it inline; the underlying file can remain at the default `$CODEX_HOME/*` path.
- Never leave a project-referenced asset only at the default `$CODEX_HOME/*` path.
- Do not overwrite an existing asset unless the user explicitly asked for replacement; otherwise create a sibling versioned filename such as `hero-v2.png` or `item-icon-edited.png`.

Shared prompt guidance for both modes lives in `references/prompting.md` and `references/sample-prompts.md`.

Fallback-only docs/resources for CLI mode:
- `references/cli.md`
- `references/image-api.md`
- `references/codex-network.md`
- `scripts/image_gen.py`

Local post-processing helper:
- `$CODEX_HOME/skills/.system/imagegen/scripts/remove_chroma_key.py`: removes a flat chroma-key background from a generated image and writes a PNG/WebP with alpha. Prefer auto-key sampling, soft matte, and despill for antialiased edges.

## When to use
- Generate a new image (concept art, product shot, cover, website hero)
- Generate a new image using one or more reference images for style, composition, or mood
- Edit an existing image (inpainting, lighting or weather transformations, background replacement, object removal, compositing, transparent background)
- Produce many assets or variants for one task

## When not to use
- Extending or matching an existing SVG/vector icon set, logo system, or illustration library inside the repo
- Creating simple shapes, diagrams, wireframes, or icons that are better produced directly in SVG, HTML/CSS, or canvas
- Making a small project-local asset edit when the source file already exists in an editable native format
- Any task where the user clearly wants deterministic code-native output instead of a generated bitmap

## Decision tree

Think about two separate questions:

1. **Intent:** is this a new image or an edit of an existing image?
2. **Execution strategy:** is this one asset or many assets/variants?

Intent:
- If the user wants to modify an existing image while preserving parts of it, treat the request as **edit**.
- If the user provides images only as references for style, composition, mood, or subject guidance, treat the request as **generate**.
- If the user provides no images, treat the request as **generate**.

Built-in edit semantics:
- Built-in edit mode is for images already visible in the conversation context, such as attached images or images generated earlier in the thread.
- If the user wants to edit a local image file with the built-in tool, first load it with built-in `view_image` tool so the image is visible in the conversation context, then proceed with the built-in edit flow.
- Do not promise arbitrary filesystem-path editing through the built-in tool.
- If a local file still needs direct file-path control, masks, or other explicit CLI-only parameters, use the explicit CLI fallback only when the user asks for it.
- For edits, preserve invariants aggressively and save non-destructively by default.

Execution strategy:
- In the built-in default path, produce many assets or variants by issuing one `image_gen` call per requested asset or variant.
- In the CLI fallback path, use the CLI `generate-batch` subcommand only when the user explicitly chose CLI mode and needs many prompts/assets.
- For many distinct assets, do not use `n` as a substitute for separate prompts. `n` is for variants of one prompt; distinct assets need distinct built-in calls or distinct CLI `generate-batch` jobs.

Assume the user wants a new image unless they clearly ask to change an existing one.

## Workflow
1. Decide the top-level mode: built-in by default, including simple transparent-output requests; fallback CLI only if explicitly requested or after the user explicitly confirms a transparent-output fallback.
2. Decide the intent: `generate` or `edit`.
3. Decide whether the output is preview-only or meant to be consumed by the current project.
4. Decide the execution strategy: single asset vs repeated built-in calls vs CLI `generate-batch`.
5. Collect inputs up front: prompt(s), exact text (verbatim), constraints/avoid list, and any input images.
6. For every input image, label its role explicitly:
   - reference image
   - edit target
   - supporting insert/style/compositing input
7. If the edit target is only on the local filesystem and you are staying on the built-in path, inspect it with `view_image` first so the image is available in conversation context.
8. If the user asked for a photo, illustration, sprite, product image, banner, or other explicitly raster-style asset, use `image_gen` rather than substituting SVG/HTML/CSS placeholders. If the request is for an icon, logo, or UI graphic that should match existing repo-native SVG/vector/code assets, prefer editing those directly instead.
9. Augment the prompt based on specificity:
   - If the user's prompt is already specific and detailed, normalize it into a clear spec without adding creative requirements.
   - If the user's prompt is generic, add tasteful augmentation only when it materially improves output quality.
10. Use the built-in `image_gen` tool by default.
11. For transparent-output requests, follow the transparent image guidance below: generate with built-in `image_gen` on a flat chroma-key background, copy the selected output into the workspace or `tmp/imagegen/`, run the installed `$CODEX_HOME/skills/.system/imagegen/scripts/remove_chroma_key.py` helper, and validate the alpha result before using it. If this path looks unsuitable or fails, ask before switching to CLI `gpt-image-1.5`.
12. Inspect outputs and validate: subject, style, composition, text accuracy, and invariants/avoid items.
13. Iterate with a single targeted change, then re-check.
14. For preview-only work, render the image inline; the underlying file may remain at the default `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...` path.
15. For project-bound work, move or copy the selected artifact into the workspace and update any consuming code or references. Never leave a project-referenced asset only at the default `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...` path.
16. For batches or multi-asset requests, persist every requested deliverable final in the workspace unless the user explicitly asked to keep outputs preview-only. Discarded variants do not need to be kept unless requested.
17. If the user explicitly chooses or confirms the CLI fallback, then use the fallback-only docs for model, quality, size, `input_fidelity`, masks, output format, output paths, and network setup.
18. Always report the final saved path(s) for any workspace-bound asset(s), plus the final prompt or prompt set and whether the built-in tool or fallback CLI mode was used.

## Transparent image requests

Transparent-image requests still use built-in `image_gen` first. Because the built-in tool does not expose a true transparent-background control, create a removable chroma-key source image and then convert the key color to alpha locally.

Default sequence:
1. Use built-in `image_gen` to generate the requested subject on a perfectly flat solid chroma-key background.
2. Choose a key color that is unlikely to appear in the subject: default `#00ff00`, use `#ff00ff` for green subjects, and avoid `#0000ff` for blue subjects.
3. After generation, move or copy the selected source image from `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...` into the workspace or `tmp/imagegen/`.
4. Run the installed helper path, not a project-relative script path:
   ```bash
   python "${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/skills/.system/imagegen/scripts/remove_chroma_key.py" \
     --input <source> \
     --out <final.png> \
     --auto-key border \
     --soft-matte \
     --transparent-threshold 12 \
     --opaque-threshold 220 \
     --despill
   ```
5. Validate that the output has an alpha channel, transparent corners, plausible subject coverage, and no obvious key-color fringe. If a thin fringe remains, retry once with `--edge-contract 1`; use `--edge-feather 0.25` only when the edge is visibly stair-stepped and the subject is not shiny or reflective.
6. Save the final alpha PNG/WebP in the project if the asset is project-bound. Never leave a project-referenced transparent asset only under `$CODEX_HOME/*`.

Prompt transparent requests like this:

```text
Create the requested subject on a perfectly flat solid #00ff00 chroma-key background for background removal.
The background must be one uniform color with no shadows, gradients, texture, reflections, floor plane, or lighting variation.
Keep the subject fully separated from the background with crisp edges and generous padding.
Do not use #00ff00 anywhere in the subject.
No cast shadow, no contact shadow, no reflection, no watermark, and no text unless explicitly requested.
```

Do not automatically use CLI `gpt-image-1.5 --background transparent --output-format png` instead of chroma keying. Ask the user first when the user asks for true/native transparency, when local removal fails validation, or when the requested image is complex: hair, fur, feathers, smoke, glass, liquids, translucent materials, reflective objects, soft shadows, realistic product grounding, or subject colors that conflict with all practical key colors.

Use a concise confirmation like:

```text
This likely needs true native transparency. The default built-in path uses a chroma-key background plus local removal, but true transparency requires the CLI fallback with gpt-image-1.5 because gpt-image-2 does not support background=transparent. It also requires OPENAI_API_KEY. Should I proceed with that CLI fallback?
```

## Prompt augmentation

Reformat user prompts into a structured, production-oriented spec. Make the user's goal clearer and more actionable, but do not blindly add detail.

Treat this as prompt-shaping guidance, not a closed schema. Use only the lines that help, and add a short extra labeled line when it materially improves clarity.

### Specificity policy

Use the user's prompt specificity to decide how much augmentation is appropriate:

- If the prompt is already specific and detailed, preserve that specificity and only normalize/structure it.
- If the prompt is generic, you may add tasteful augmentation when it will materially improve the result.

Allowed augmentations:
- composition or framing hints
- polish level or intended-use hints
- practical layout guidance
- reasonable scene concreteness that supports the stated request

Not allowed augmentations:
- extra characters or objects that are not implied by the request
- brand names, slogans, palettes, or narrative beats that are not implied
- arbitrary side-specific placement unless the surrounding layout supports it

## Use-case taxonomy (exact slugs)

Classify each request into one of these buckets and keep the slug consistent across prompts and references.

Generate:
- photorealistic-natural — candid/editorial lifestyle scenes with real texture and natural lighting.
- product-mockup — product/packaging shots, catalog imagery, merch concepts.
- ui-mockup — app/web interface mockups and wireframes; specify the desired fidelity.
- infographic-diagram — diagrams/infographics with structured layout and text.
- scientific-educational — classroom explainers, scientific diagrams, and learning visuals with required labels and accuracy constraints.
- ads-marketing — campaign concepts and ad creatives with audience, brand position, scene, and exact tagline/copy.
- productivity-visual — slide, chart, workflow, and data-heavy business visuals.
- logo-brand — logo/mark exploration, vector-friendly.
- illustration-story — comics, children’s book art, narrative scenes.
- stylized-concept — style-driven concept art, 3D/stylized renders.
- historical-scene — period-accurate/world-knowledge scenes.

Edit:
- text-localization — translate/replace in-image text, preserve layout.
- identity-preserve — try-on, person-in-scene; lock face/body/pose.
- precise-object-edit — remove/replace a specific element (including interior swaps).
- lighting-weather — time-of-day/season/atmosphere changes only.
- background-extraction — transparent background / clean cutout. Use built-in `image_gen` with chroma-key removal first for simple opaque subjects; ask before using CLI true transparency for complex subjects.
- style-transfer — apply reference style while changing subject/scene.
- compositing — multi-image insert/merge with matched lighting/perspective.
- sketch-to-render — drawing/line art to photoreal render.

## Shared prompt schema

Use the following labeled spec as shared prompt scaffolding for both top-level modes:

```text
Use case: <taxonomy slug>
Asset type: <where the asset will be used>
Primary request: <user's main prompt>
Input images: <Image 1: role; Image 2: role> (optional)
Scene/backdrop: <environment>
Subject: <main subject>
Style/medium: <photo/illustration/3D/etc>
Composition/framing: <wide/close/top-down; placement>
Lighting/mood: <lighting + mood>
Color palette: <palette notes>
Materials/textures: <surface details>
Text (verbatim): "<exact text>"
Constraints: <must keep/must avoid>
Avoid: <negative constraints>
```

Notes:
- `Asset type` and `Input images` are prompt scaffolding, not dedicated CLI flags.
- `Scene/backdrop` refers to the visual setting. It is not the same as the fallback CLI `background` parameter, which controls output transparency behavior.
- Fallback-only execution notes such as `Quality:`, `Input fidelity:`, masks, output format, and output paths belong in the CLI path only. Do not treat them as built-in `image_gen` tool arguments.

Augmentation rules:
- Keep it short.
- Add only the details needed to improve the prompt materially.
- For edits, explicitly list invariants (`change only X; keep Y unchanged`).
- If any critical detail is missing and blocks success, ask a question; otherwise proceed.

# Image Generation Skill

Generates or edits images for the current project (for example website assets, game assets, UI mockups, product mockups, wireframes, logo design, photorealistic images, or infographics).

## Top-level modes and rules

This skill has exactly two top-level modes:

- **Default built-in tool mode (preferred):** built-in `image_gen` tool for normal image generation, editing, and simple transparent-image requests. Does not require `OPENAI_API_KEY`.
- **Fallback CLI mode:** `scripts/image_gen.py` CLI. Use when the user explicitly asks for the CLI/API/model path, or after the user explicitly confirms a true model-native transparency fallback with `gpt-image-1.5`. Requires `OPENAI_API_KEY`.

Within CLI fallback, the CLI exposes three subcommands:

- `generate`
- `edit`
- `generate-batch`

Rules:
- Use the built-in `image_gen` tool by default for normal image generation and editing requests.
- Do not switch to CLI fallback for ordinary quality, size, or file-path control.
- If the user explicitly asks for a transparent image/background, stay on built-in `image_gen` first: prompt for a flat removable chroma-key background, then remove it locally with the installed helper at `$CODEX_HOME/skills/.system/imagegen/scripts/remove_chroma_key.py`.
- Never silently switch from built-in `image_gen` or CLI `gpt-image-2` to CLI `gpt-image-1.5`. Treat this as a model/path downgrade and ask the user before doing it, unless the user has already explicitly requested `gpt-image-1.5`, `scripts/image_gen.py`, or CLI fallback.
- If a transparent request appears too complex for clean chroma-key removal, asks for true/native transparency, or local removal fails validation, explain that true transparency requires CLI `gpt-image-1.5 --background transparent --output-format png` because `gpt-image-2` does not support `background=transparent`, then ask whether to proceed. Run the CLI fallback only after the user confirms.
- The word `batch` by itself does not mean CLI fallback. If the user asks for many assets or says to batch-generate assets without explicitly asking for CLI/API/model controls, stay on the built-in path and issue one built-in call per requested asset or variant.
- If the built-in tool fails or is unavailable, tell the user the CLI fallback exists and that it requires `OPENAI_API_KEY`. Proceed only if the user explicitly asks for that fallback.
- If the user explicitly asks for CLI mode, use the bundled `scripts/image_gen.py` workflow. Do not create one-off SDK runners.
- Never modify `scripts/image_gen.py`. If something is missing, ask the user before doing anything else.

Built-in save-path policy:
- In built-in tool mode, Codex saves generated images under `$CODEX_HOME/*` by default.
- Do not describe or rely on OS temp as the default built-in destination.
- Do not describe or rely on a destination-path argument (if any) on the built-in `image_gen` tool. If a specific location is needed, generate first and then move or copy the selected output from `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...`.
- Save-path precedence in built-in mode:
  1. If the user names a destination, move or copy the selected output there.
  2. If the image is meant for the current project, move or copy the final selected image into the workspace before finishing.
  3. If the image is only for preview or brainstorming, render it inline; the underlying file can remain at the default `$CODEX_HOME/*` path.
- Never leave a project-referenced asset only at the default `$CODEX_HOME/*` path.
- Do not overwrite an existing asset unless the user explicitly asked for replacement; otherwise create a sibling versioned filename such as `hero-v2.png` or `item-icon-edited.png`.

Shared prompt guidance for both modes lives in `references/prompting.md` and `references/sample-prompts.md`.

Fallback-only docs/resources for CLI mode:
- `references/cli.md`
- `references/image-api.md`
- `references/codex-network.md`
- `scripts/image_gen.py`

Local post-processing helper:
- `$CODEX_HOME/skills/.system/imagegen/scripts/remove_chroma_key.py`: removes a flat chroma-key background from a generated image and writes a PNG/WebP with alpha. Prefer auto-key sampling, soft matte, and despill for antialiased edges.

## When to use
- Generate a new image (concept art, product shot, cover, website hero)
- Generate a new image using one or more reference images for style, composition, or mood
- Edit an existing image (inpainting, lighting or weather transformations, background replacement, object removal, compositing, transparent background)
- Produce many assets or variants for one task

## When not to use
- Extending or matching an existing SVG/vector icon set, logo system, or illustration library inside the repo
- Creating simple shapes, diagrams, wireframes, or icons that are better produced directly in SVG, HTML/CSS, or canvas
- Making a small project-local asset edit when the source file already exists in an editable native format
- Any task where the user clearly wants deterministic code-native output instead of a generated bitmap

## Decision tree

Think about two separate questions:

1. **Intent:** is this a new image or an edit of an existing image?
2. **Execution strategy:** is this one asset or many assets/variants?

Intent:
- If the user wants to modify an existing image while preserving parts of it, treat the request as **edit**.
- If the user provides images only as references for style, composition, mood, or subject guidance, treat the request as **generate**.
- If the user provides no images, treat the request as **generate**.

Built-in edit semantics:
- Built-in edit mode is for images already visible in the conversation context, such as attached images or images generated earlier in the thread.
- If the user wants to edit a local image file with the built-in tool, first load it with built-in `view_image` tool so the image is visible in the conversation context, then proceed with the built-in edit flow.
- Do not promise arbitrary filesystem-path editing through the built-in tool.
- If a local file still needs direct file-path control, masks, or other explicit CLI-only parameters, use the explicit CLI fallback only when the user asks for it.
- For edits, preserve invariants aggressively and save non-destructively by default.

Execution strategy:
- In the built-in default path, produce many assets or variants by issuing one `image_gen` call per requested asset or variant.
- In the CLI fallback path, use the CLI `generate-batch` subcommand only when the user explicitly chose CLI mode and needs many prompts/assets.
- For many distinct assets, do not use `n` as a substitute for separate prompts. `n` is for variants of one prompt; distinct assets need distinct built-in calls or distinct CLI `generate-batch` jobs.

Assume the user wants a new image unless they clearly ask to change an existing one.

## Workflow
1. Decide the top-level mode: built-in by default, including simple transparent-output requests; fallback CLI only if explicitly requested or after the user explicitly confirms a transparent-output fallback.
2. Decide the intent: `generate` or `edit`.
3. Decide whether the output is preview-only or meant to be consumed by the current project.
4. Decide the execution strategy: single asset vs repeated built-in calls vs CLI `generate-batch`.
5. Collect inputs up front: prompt(s), exact text (verbatim), constraints/avoid list, and any input images.
6. For every input image, label its role explicitly:
   - reference image
   - edit target
   - supporting insert/style/compositing input
7. If the edit target is only on the local filesystem and you are staying on the built-in path, inspect it with `view_image` first so the image is available in conversation context.
8. If the user asked for a photo, illustration, sprite, product image, banner, or other explicitly raster-style asset, use `image_gen` rather than substituting SVG/HTML/CSS placeholders. If the request is for an icon, logo, or UI graphic that should match existing repo-native SVG/vector/code assets, prefer editing those directly instead.
9. Augment the prompt based on specificity:
   - If the user's prompt is already specific and detailed, normalize it into a clear spec without adding creative requirements.
   - If the user's prompt is generic, add tasteful augmentation only when it materially improves output quality.
10. Use the built-in `image_gen` tool by default.
11. For transparent-output requests, follow the transparent image guidance below: generate with built-in `image_gen` on a flat chroma-key background, copy the selected output into the workspace or `tmp/imagegen/`, run the installed `$CODEX_HOME/skills/.system/imagegen/scripts/remove_chroma_key.py` helper, and validate the alpha result before using it. If this path looks unsuitable or fails, ask before switching to CLI `gpt-image-1.5`.
12. Inspect outputs and validate: subject, style, composition, text accuracy, and invariants/avoid items.
13. Iterate with a single targeted change, then re-check.
14. For preview-only work, render the image inline; the underlying file may remain at the default `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...` path.
15. For project-bound work, move or copy the selected artifact into the workspace and update any consuming code or references. Never leave a project-referenced asset only at the default `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...` path.
16. For batches or multi-asset requests, persist every requested deliverable final in the workspace unless the user explicitly asked to keep outputs preview-only. Discarded variants do not need to be kept unless requested.
17. If the user explicitly chooses or confirms the CLI fallback, then use the fallback-only docs for model, quality, size, `input_fidelity`, masks, output format, output paths, and network setup.
18. Always report the final saved path(s) for any workspace-bound asset(s), plus the final prompt or prompt set and whether the built-in tool or fallback CLI mode was used.

## Transparent image requests

Transparent-image requests still use built-in `image_gen` first. Because the built-in tool does not expose a true transparent-background control, create a removable chroma-key source image and then convert the key color to alpha locally.

Default sequence:
1. Use built-in `image_gen` to generate the requested subject on a perfectly flat solid chroma-key background.
2. Choose a key color that is unlikely to appear in the subject: default `#00ff00`, use `#ff00ff` for green subjects, and avoid `#0000ff` for blue subjects.
3. After generation, move or copy the selected source image from `$CODEX_HOME/generated_images/...` into the workspace or `tmp/imagegen/`.
4. Run the installed helper path, not a project-relative script path:
   ```bash
   python "${CODEX_HOME:-$HOME/.codex}/skills/.system/imagegen/scripts/remove_chroma_key.py" \
     --input <source> \
     --out <final.png> \
     --auto-key border \
     --soft-matte \
     --transparent-threshold 12 \
     --opaque-threshold 220 \
     --despill
   ```
5. Validate that the output has an alpha channel, transparent corners, plausible subject coverage, and no obvious key-color fringe. If a thin fringe remains, retry once with `--edge-contract 1`; use `--edge-feather 0.25` only when the edge is visibly stair-stepped and the subject is not shiny or reflective.
6. Save the final alpha PNG/WebP in the project if the asset is project-bound. Never leave a project-referenced transparent asset only under `$CODEX_HOME/*`.

Prompt transparent requests like this:

```text
Create the requested subject on a perfectly flat solid #00ff00 chroma-key background for background removal.
The background must be one uniform color with no shadows, gradients, texture, reflections, floor plane, or lighting variation.
Keep the subject fully separated from the background with crisp edges and generous padding.
Do not use #00ff00 anywhere in the subject.
No cast shadow, no contact shadow, no reflection, no watermark, and no text unless explicitly requested.
```

Do not automatically use CLI `gpt-image-1.5 --background transparent --output-format png` instead of chroma keying. Ask the user first when the user asks for true/native transparency, when local removal fails validation, or when the requested image is complex: hair, fur, feathers, smoke, glass, liquids, translucent materials, reflective objects, soft shadows, realistic product grounding, or subject colors that conflict with all practical key colors.

Use a concise confirmation like:

```text
This likely needs true native transparency. The default built-in path uses a chroma-key background plus local removal, but true transparency requires the CLI fallback with gpt-image-1.5 because gpt-image-2 does not support background=transparent. It also requires OPENAI_API_KEY. Should I proceed with that CLI fallback?
```

## Prompt augmentation

Reformat user prompts into a structured, production-oriented spec. Make the user's goal clearer and more actionable, but do not blindly add detail.

Treat this as prompt-shaping guidance, not a closed schema. Use only the lines that help, and add a short extra labeled line when it materially improves clarity.

### Specificity policy

Use the user's prompt specificity to decide how much augmentation is appropriate:

- If the prompt is already specific and detailed, preserve that specificity and only normalize/structure it.
- If the prompt is generic, you may add tasteful augmentation when it will materially improve the result.

Allowed augmentations:
- composition or framing hints
- polish level or intended-use hints
- practical layout guidance
- reasonable scene concreteness that supports the stated request

Not allowed augmentations:
- extra characters or objects that are not implied by the request
- brand names, slogans, palettes, or narrative beats that are not implied
- arbitrary side-specific placement unless the surrounding layout supports it

## Use-case taxonomy (exact slugs)

Classify each request into one of these buckets and keep the slug consistent across prompts and references.

Generate:
- photorealistic-natural — candid/editorial lifestyle scenes with real texture and natural lighting.
- product-mockup — product/packaging shots, catalog imagery, merch concepts.
- ui-mockup — app/web interface mockups and wireframes; specify the desired fidelity.
- infographic-diagram — diagrams/infographics with structured layout and text.
- scientific-educational — classroom explainers, scientific diagrams, and learning visuals with required labels and accuracy constraints.
- ads-marketing — campaign concepts and ad creatives with audience, brand position, scene, and exact tagline/copy.
- productivity-visual — slide, chart, workflow, and data-heavy business visuals.
- logo-brand — logo/mark exploration, vector-friendly.
- illustration-story — comics, children’s book art, narrative scenes.
- stylized-concept — style-driven concept art, 3D/stylized renders.
- historical-scene — period-accurate/world-knowledge scenes.

Edit:
- text-localization — translate/replace in-image text, preserve layout.
- identity-preserve — try-on, person-in-scene; lock face/body/pose.
- precise-object-edit — remove/replace a specific element (including interior swaps).
- lighting-weather — time-of-day/season/atmosphere changes only.
- background-extraction — transparent background / clean cutout. Use built-in `image_gen` with chroma-key removal first for simple opaque subjects; ask before using CLI true transparency for complex subjects.
- style-transfer — apply reference style while changing subject/scene.
- compositing — multi-image insert/merge with matched lighting/perspective.
- sketch-to-render — drawing/line art to photoreal render.

## Shared prompt schema

Use the following labeled spec as shared prompt scaffolding for both top-level modes:

```text
Use case: <taxonomy slug>
Asset type: <where the asset will be used>
Primary request: <user's main prompt>
Input images: <Image 1: role; Image 2: role> (optional)
Scene/backdrop: <environment>
Subject: <main subject>

```

# Video Agent File Workflow

## Class of task

Use this skill when a user wants Hermes to act as a video generation producer/orchestrator for a file-based, human-in-the-loop creative pipeline: create or resume a session, generate stage artifacts, run automated review, stop for human approval, and validate machine-readable outputs.

## Trigger phrases

Load this skill when the user asks to:

- “作为视频生成 Agent 制片人主控工作”
- “创建新视频生成会话”
- “继续 {session_id}” for a video session
- “执行到 brief/script/storyboard/assets 阶段”
- “批准 brief/script/storyboard/assets/shots，进入下一阶段”
- “接入视频片段生成 skill / Doubao Seedance / 火山方舟视频生成接口”
- “根据 storyboard 和 assets 组织视频生成 API 调用参数”
- “提交视频生成任务 / 提交 Doubao Seedance 任务 / 逐个 shot 生成”
- “使用即梦 CLI / dreamina CLI / 剪映即梦生成视频片段”
- “把 Dreamina CLI / 即梦官方 skill 安装到 video-agent 里”
- “一个 shot 生成完并确认效果后再继续下一个 shot”
- “跳过后面的 shots，先用已经生成的 clips 测试混剪 / edit skill”
- “用部分已生成 shot 做 rough cut / partial edit test”
- “验证第一个 demo 是否跑通”
- “检查 production_state.json / review gate / stale 产物”
- “业务用户看不懂太多 JSON/英文/文件，想优化可视化体验”
- “希望在当前对话框中直接看到剧本、分镜表、分镜图、视频片段”
- “给 video-agent 做业务审核面板 / dashboard / current_review”
- “把剧本、分镜、资产、shots、视频效果审核改成中文卡片/表格展示”

## Project location

For the user's Hermes project, the video agent workspace is:

```text
/Users/edy/vedio-agent/hermes-agent-main/video-agent/
```

All video-agent artifacts must remain under that workspace. The recommended per-session root now uses a two-level topic/session layout:

```text
video-agent/video-generation-session/{topic_name}/{session_id}/
```

`session_id` format:

```text
{topic_name}_no{episode_number}_{yyyymmdd}
```

Example:

```text
video-agent/video-generation-session/cyber_cat_trailer/cyber_cat_trailer_no1_20260503/
video-agent/video-generation-session/cyber_cat_trailer/cyber_cat_trailer_no2_20260506/
```

Legacy flat sessions such as `video-agent/video-generation-session/cyber_cat_trailer_20260503/` remain recoverable, but new themes and sequels should default to the two-level layout.

## Required files to read first

Before creating, resuming, or advancing a video session, read the current project-local source of truth:

```text
video-agent/README.md
video-agent/skills/video-producer/SKILL.md
```

For stage execution, also read the relevant schemas/templates/reviewer docs:

```text
video-agent/templates/production-state-template.json
video-agent/templates/user-brief-template.md
video-agent/schemas/production-state.schema.json
video-agent/schemas/brief.schema.json
video-agent/schemas/script.schema.json
video-agent/schemas/storyboard.schema.json
video-agent/schemas/assets-manifest.schema.json
video-agent/schemas/shot-generation-tasks.schema.json
video-agent/schemas/shot-submission.schema.json
video-agent/schemas/dreamina-shot-submission.schema.json
video-agent/schemas/edit-manifest.schema.json
video-agent/schemas/review.schema.json
video-agent/skills/mock-scriptwriter/SKILL.md
video-agent/skills/mock-storyboard/SKILL.md
video-agent/skills/mock-asset-planner/SKILL.md
video-agent/skills/doubao-seedance-shot-generator/SKILL.md
video-agent/skills/doubao-seedance-shot-submitter/SKILL.md
video-agent/skills/dreamina-shot-submitter/SKILL.md
video-agent/skills/simple-video-editor/SKILL.md
video-agent/skills/reviewers/*.md
```

Only read stage-specific files as needed to avoid unnecessary context, but always read README and video-producer first.

## Core workflow rules

1. The producer agent owns all user interaction; subskills/reviewers do not ask the user directly.
2. Every session has one `production_state.json` as the source of truth.
3. Every key stage follows:

```text
generate artifact -> automated review -> human review -> approve/revise -> next stage
```

4. Never enter the next stage before human approval.
5. If an upstream artifact changes, mark downstream artifacts as `stale` and do not use stale artifacts to advance.
6. Save human-readable artifacts as Markdown/CSV and machine-readable artifacts as JSON.
7. Validate all JSON outputs against their schemas before asking for human approval.
8. Automated review can request human review only when:

```text
verdict = pass
blocking_issues = []
```

## Creating a new session

When the user provides a topic name and request:

1. Normalize the topic name into `topic_name` using lowercase English, numbers, `_`, and `-`.
2. Determine the episode number:
   - for a new theme, default to `no1`;
   - for a sequel/continuation under the same theme, use the next available number such as `no2`;
   - if the next number is ambiguous, ask the user instead of overwriting an existing session.
3. Use the current date from the system (`date +%Y%m%d`) to build `session_id = {topic_name}_no{episode_number}_{yyyymmdd}`.
4. The session root should be `video-agent/video-generation-session/{topic_name}/{session_id}/`.
5. Check whether the session directory exists and is non-empty; do not overwrite silently.
6. Create directories:

```text
brief/
script/
storyboard/
assets/
reviews/
reports/
```

7. Initialize `production_state.json`.
8. Create `reviews/human_review_log.md` and `reports/production_log.md`.
9. Generate only the requested stage; if the user says “只执行到 brief 阶段”, stop after brief review and human-review request.

## Brief stage checklist

Expected artifacts:

```text
production_state.json
brief/user_brief.md
brief/normalized_brief.json
brief/review_v001.md
brief/review_v001.json
reviews/human_review_log.md
reports/production_log.md
```

Validation:

- `production_state.json` parses and validates against `production-state.schema.json`.
- `brief/normalized_brief.json` parses and validates against `brief.schema.json`.
- `brief/review_v001.json` parses and validates against `review.schema.json`.
- `production_state.json.current_stage == "brief"`.
- `stages.brief.status == "pending_human_review"` after review passes.
- `script`, `storyboard`, `assets`, and `package` remain `not_started`.
- `review_v001.json.verdict == "pass"` and `blocking_issues == []` before asking the user to approve.

## Storyboard stage checklist

When the user approves script and asks to enter storyboard:

1. Re-read `production_state.json`, the approved script JSON, the script review JSON, `storyboard.schema.json`, `review.schema.json`, `mock-storyboard/SKILL.md`, and `storyboard-reviewer.md`.
2. Confirm the script stage is currently `pending_human_review`, its automated review is `verdict=pass` with `blocking_issues=[]`, and the brief stage is already `approved`.
3. Record exactly the script approval in both `production_state.json.human_approvals` and `reviews/human_review_log.md`; do not duplicate earlier brief approvals.
4. Generate:

```text
storyboard/storyboard_v001.md
storyboard/storyboard_v001.json
storyboard/storyboard_v001.csv
storyboard/review_v001.md
storyboard/review_v001.json
```

5. The storyboard should cover every script scene and beat, keep `actual_duration_sec` within `duration_tolerance_sec`, include `asset_requirements` and generation/negative prompts for every shot, and preserve continuity constraints from the approved script.
6. If an upstream reviewer gave a non-blocking quality suggestion, incorporate it in storyboard design where possible. Example: if a future hook has too much text, make it a brief visual flicker or image beat rather than an explanatory subtitle.
7. Validate `production_state.json`, `storyboard_v001.json`, and `storyboard/review_v001.json` against their schemas; also verify scene/beat references and CSV/JSON consistency.
8. Stop at `stages.storyboard.status == "pending_human_review"`; leave `assets`, `shots`, `edit`, and `package` as `not_started` unless the user explicitly approves storyboard later.
9. Read back `storyboard_v001.md`, `storyboard/review_v001.md`, and the relevant `production_state.json` lines before responding.

## Assets stage checklist

When the user approves storyboard and asks to enter assets:

1. Re-read `production_state.json`, the approved storyboard JSON, the storyboard review JSON, the approved script JSON, `assets-manifest.schema.json`, `review.schema.json`, `mock-asset-planner/SKILL.md`, and `asset-reviewer.md`.
2. Confirm the storyboard stage is currently `pending_human_review`, its review gate is `verdict=pass` with `blocking_issues=[]`, and the script stage is already `approved`.
3. Record exactly the stage being approved now. Do not duplicate approvals for earlier stages that are already approved; if a preserved todo or handoff mentions an older approval again, treat it as stale task-list wording and continue from the actual `production_state.json`.
4. Generate:

```text
assets/assets_manifest_v001.md
assets/assets_manifest_v001.json
assets/review_v001.md
assets/review_v001.json
```

5. The assets manifest should cover all script characters, all script scenes, every storyboard shot, and every `storyboard.shots[].asset_requirements[]` item. `coverage.unresolved_asset_requirements` must be empty before requesting human review.
6. Preserve continuity constraints as asset-level rules. If a prior revision distinguished a clue/projection from a physical object, represent them as separate assets so downstream generators cannot reuse the wrong one in early scenes.
7. Validate `production_state.json`, `assets_manifest_v001.json`, and `assets/review_v001.json` against their schemas.
8. Stop at `stages.assets.status == "pending_human_review"`; leave `package`, `shots`, and `edit` as `not_started` unless the user explicitly approves assets later.
9. Read back `assets_manifest_v001.md`, `assets/review_v001.md`, and the relevant `production_state.json` lines before responding.

## Shots request-planning checklist

When the user has an approved storyboard and assets manifest and asks to connect a video clip generation API/skill (for example Doubao Seedance / 火山方舟), treat this as a `shots` request-planning stage before any real submission:

1. Re-read `production_state.json`, approved `storyboard/storyboard_v{N}.json`, approved `assets/assets_manifest_v{N}.json`, `shot-generation-tasks.schema.json`, `review.schema.json`, the shot generator skill doc, and the shot-task reviewer doc.
2. Confirm `stages.storyboard.status == "approved"`, `stages.assets.status == "approved"`, both are not stale, and the versions match `production_state.json.versions`.
3. Generate request-package artifacts without calling the external API:

```text
shots/shot_tasks_v001.md
shots/shot_tasks_v001.json
shots/review_v001.md
shots/review_v001.json
```

4. For Doubao Seedance request packages, preserve the provider contract in JSON: endpoint, model, `content` array, text prompt, optional reference image/video/audio items, `generate_audio`, `ratio`, `duration`, and `watermark`.
5. Never write real credentials into artifacts. Use `Bearer ${ARK_API_KEY}` or another explicit environment-variable placeholder in headers/curl templates, and read the real value only in a later submitter/executor stage.
6. Keep the shot generator's default responsibility narrow: it organizes API parameters and curl/request templates; it does not submit jobs unless the user explicitly asks for a submitter/execution stage.
7. Validate `shot_tasks_v001.json` against `shot-generation-tasks.schema.json` and validate the shot review JSON against `review.schema.json`.
8. Ensure coverage is complete: every storyboard shot has one task, `coverage.missing_shot_ids` is empty, and all referenced asset keys exist in the assets manifest or are explicitly marked unresolved.
9. Stop at `stages.shots.status == "pending_human_review"` if the review gate passes. Ask the user to approve shots before submitting real video-generation jobs.
10. If a production package was already generated before inserting or regenerating the shots stage, mark the package `stale` with a clear reason so the old package is not treated as final.

## Sequential shot submission checklist

When the user approves a shot request package and asks to submit video generation jobs, treat this as a sequential execution subflow under the `shots` stage:

1. Re-read `production_state.json`, `shots/shot_tasks_v{N}.json`, `shots/review_v{N}.json`, `shot-submission.schema.json`, and the shot submitter skill doc.
2. Confirm the request package review gate is `verdict=pass` and `blocking_issues=[]`, and record the user's approval for `shots v{N}` in both `production_state.json.human_approvals` and `reviews/human_review_log.md`.
3. Enforce `one_shot_at_a_time`: submit only the lowest-order shot not already human-approved. Never submit shot N+1 while shot N is submitted, pending generation, pending human effect review, failed, or rejected.
4. Read credentials only from the runtime environment, e.g. `ARK_API_KEY`. Never write the token into JSON, Markdown, curl templates, logs, or final responses.
5. Write provider responses under:

```text
shots/provider_responses/{shot_id}_submit_response_v{N}.json
```

6. Track the sequential queue in:

```

name: asset-planner

metadata:

---

# Asset Planner Skill

## 1. Overview

`asset-planner` is the Video Agent asset-stage skill. It sits after the upstream scriptwriter and storyboard skills:

```text
scriptwriter -> storyboard -> asset-planner -> asset-reviewer -> shot/video generation
```

Its job is not only to make a mock manifest. It must use the approved script and approved storyboard together to:

1. Extract reusable production assets: characters, character turnarounds, scenes/backgrounds, props, UI/FX cues, style references, visual systems, and shot-critical image needs.
2. Write a strict schema-valid asset manifest:
   ```text
   {session_root}/assets/assets_manifest_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
   {session_root}/assets/assets_manifest_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.md
   ```
3. Optionally generate actual reusable asset reference images and save them inside the current session assets folder.
4. Generate a complete set of cinematic storyboard frame images for every storyboard shot, using the script, storyboard, and generated asset references as continuity anchors.
5. Keep all asset-stage outputs inside:
   ```text
   {session_root}/assets/
   ```

The machine-readable asset manifest must validate against:

```text
video-agent/schemas/assets-manifest.schema.json
```

Do not use the old standalone image-storyboard project layout (`project_manifest.json`, `storyboard-images/`, `managed-photos/`, `deliverables/`) inside Video Agent sessions. In Video Agent, asset reference images and storyboard frame images both live under the session `assets/` tree.

## 2. When to Use

Use this skill when:

- The script stage and storyboard stage have both been approved.
- The user asks to extract assets from the script and storyboard.
- The user asks for character, scene, prop, visual-style, UI/FX, or frame reference images before video generation.
- The user asks to generate a full set of realistic cinematic storyboard images from the approved script/storyboard.
- A downstream shot/video generator needs `assets/assets_manifest_v{N}.json` plus local image references for consistent image-to-video or multiframe workflows.
- Script or storyboard changes make previous assets stale and the assets stage must be regenerated.

Do not use this skill for:

- Writing the script itself.
- Writing the storyboard table itself.
- Submitting video-generation jobs.
- Creating final edited videos.
- Adding non-schema fields into `assets_manifest_v{N}.json`.

## 3. Required Inputs

The producer or caller must provide a session root under:

```text
video-agent/video-generation-session/{topic_name}/{session_id}/
```

Required files:

```text
{session_root}/production_state.json
{session_root}/script/script_v{SCRIPT_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/script/script_v{SCRIPT_VERSION}.md
{session_root}/storyboard/storyboard_v{STORYBOARD_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/storyboard/storyboard_v{STORYBOARD_VERSION}.md
video-agent/schemas/script.schema.json
video-agent/schemas/storyboard.schema.json
video-agent/schemas/assets-manifest.schema.json
```

Before generating anything, verify:

- `production_state.json` exists and parses.
- `script` stage is `approved` and not `stale`.
- `storyboard` stage is `approved` and not `stale`.
- `script_v{N}.json` validates against `script.schema.json`.
- `storyboard_v{N}.json` validates against `storyboard.schema.json`.
- The script/storyboard versions match `production_state.json`.

If any prerequisite fails, stop and report the blocker. Do not create assets from stale or unapproved upstream files.

## 4. Output Contract and Paths

All outputs must stay under:

```text
{session_root}/assets/
```

### 4.1 Canonical schema outputs

Required for every asset-planner run:

```text
{session_root}/assets/assets_manifest_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/assets/assets_manifest_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.md
```

Initial version is `v001`; regeneration increments to `v002`, `v003`, etc.

### 4.2 Generated reusable asset image paths

The current `assets-manifest.schema.json` has `additionalProperties: false`. It does not define image path fields. Therefore, never add `image_path`, `local_path`, `url`, `file`, `generated_image`, or `reference_image` to `assets_manifest_v{N}.json`.

Actual reusable asset images must be copied into:

```text
{session_root}/assets/generated/{ASSETS_VERSION}/{asset_id}_{asset_key}.png
```

Examples:

```text
assets/generated/v001/asset_001_char_001_protagonist_turnaround.png
assets/generated/v001/asset_006_scene_001_rainy_alley_background.png
assets/generated/v001/asset_018_prop_001_phone_notification.png
```

Required operational sidecars for production image mode:

```text
{session_root}/assets/generated/assets_generation_queue_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/assets/generated/assets_generation_status_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
```

Optional convenience output:

```text
{session_root}/assets/generated/contact_sheet_assets_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.png
```

These sidecars are the canonical machine-readable place for local image paths because the schema JSON may not contain them. They must use session-relative paths so every other skill can resolve them by joining with `{session_root}`.

`assets_generation_queue_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json` must map every manifest asset to its target file before image generation:

```json
{
  "session_id": "topic_no1_yyyymmdd",
  "version": "v001",
  "items": [
    {
      "asset_id": "asset_001",
      "asset_key": "char_001_turnaround",
      "asset_type": "character_turnaround",
      "priority": "required",
      "target_path": "assets/generated/v001/asset_001_char_001_turnaround.png",
      "prompt": "...",
      "negative_prompt": "...",
      "status": "queued"
    }
  ]
}
```

`assets_generation_status_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json` must be updated after file verification and must be indexable by both `asset_id` and `asset_key`:

```json
{
  "session_id": "topic_no1_yyyymmdd",
  "version": "v001",
  "generated": [
    {
      "asset_id": "asset_001",
      "asset_key": "char_001_turnaround",
      "path": "assets/generated/v001/asset_001_char_001_turnaround.png",
      "size": [1024, 1536],
      "bytes": 123456,
      "qc_status": "pending_manual_qc"
    }
  ],
  "failed": [],
  "pending": []
}
```

If an image is not generated yet, leave the manifest asset `status` as `planned` and list it under `pending`; do not invent a file path as if it exists.

### 4.3 Generated full storyboard frame image paths

After reusable assets are planned or generated, generate the full storyboard image set under the same `assets/` stage:

```text
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/{ASSETS_VERSION}/{shot_id}_A.png
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/{ASSETS_VERSION}/{shot_id}_B.png
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/{ASSETS_VERSION}/{shot_id}_C.png
```

Use named variants intentionally:

```text
A = hero keyframe / most representative shot beat
B = setup, before-state, insert, or asset-context frame
C = result, after-state, reaction, or continuity-completion frame
D = optional extra detail frame when the shot cannot read in one still
```

Required storyboard-frame sidecar files when frame generation is planned or run:

```text
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frame_queue_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frame_status_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frames_manifest_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frames_manifest_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.md
```

Optional convenience output:

```text
{session_root}/assets/storyboard_frames/contact_sheet_storyboard_frames_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.png
```

These storyboard-frame sidecars are operational outputs, not replacements for `assets_manifest_v{N}.json` and not governed by `assets-manifest.schema.json` unless a dedicated storyboard-frame schema is added later. They are the canonical place where downstream shot generators should look for complete still-frame references.

`storyboard_frame_queue_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json` must list planned frames in shot order, variant order, and session-relative target paths:

```json
{
  "session_id": "topic_no1_yyyymmdd",
  "version": "v001",
  "source_storyboard_version": "v001",
  "source_assets_version": "v001",
  "frames": [
    {
      "shot_id": "shot_001",
      "variant": "A",
      "frame_id": "shot_001_A",
      "target_path": "assets/storyboard_frames/v001/shot_001_A.png",
      "asset_keys": ["char_001_turnaround", "scene_001_background"],
      "asset_image_paths": ["assets/generated/v001/asset_001_char_001_turnaround.png"],
      "prompt": "...",
      "negative_prompt": "...",
      "status": "queued"
    }
  ]
}
```

`storyboard_frame_status_v{ASSETS_VERSION}.json` must record generation/QC status with the same `frame_id`, `shot_id`, `variant`, and `path` fields:

```json
{
  "session_id": "topic_no1_yyyymmdd",
  "version": "v001",
  "frames": [
    {

```

状态更新时间我抓到了。现在我会用 Node 做一次机械更新：把已生成的 9 个资产从 `pending` 移到 `generated`，记录尺寸、字节数和 `pending_manual_qc`，并保留其余 32 个资产等待后续生成。

> TOOL

tool_use exec_command
id: call_NmmGsPREQ34k2Yi42KoJBuf1
```json
{
  "cmd": "node -e '\nconst fs=require(\"fs\");\nconst path=require(\"path\");\nconst root=\"/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/video-agent/video-generation-session/naifen_brand_creative/milkpowder_wonder_ad_20260508\";\nconst statusPath=path.join(root,\"assets/generated/assets_generation_status_v001.json\");\nconst queuePath=path.join(root,\"assets/generated/assets_generation_queue_v001.json\");\nconst status=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(statusPath,\"utf8\"));\nconst queue=JSON.parse(fs.readFileSync(queuePath,\"utf8\"));\nconst generatedIds=new Set([\"asset_001\",\"asset_002\",\"asset_004\",\"asset_007\",\"asset_010\",\"asset_023\",\"asset_026\",\"asset_031\",\"asset_041\"]);\nfunction pngSize(file){\n  const b=fs.readFileSync(file);\n  return [b.readUInt32BE(16), b.readUInt32BE(20)];\n}\nconst byId=new Map(queue.items.map(item=>[item.asset_id,item]));\nconst generated=[];\nfor(const id of [...generatedIds].sort()){\n  const item=byId.get(id);\n  if(!item) throw new Error(`Missing queue item ${id}`);\n  const abs=path.join(root,item.target_path);\n  if(!fs.existsSync(abs)) throw new Error(`Missing generated file ${abs}`);\n  const st=fs.statSync(abs);\n  generated.push({\n    asset_id:item.asset_id,\n    asset_key:item.asset_key,\n    path:item.target_path,\n    size:pngSize(abs),\n    bytes:st.size,\n    status:\"generated\",\n    qc_status:\"pending_manual_qc\",\n    generated_at:\"2026-05-08T15:32:11+08:00\"\n  });\n}\nconst existingGenerated=(status.generated||[]).filter(g=>!generatedIds.has(g.asset_id));\nstatus.generated=[...existingGenerated,...generated].sort((a,b)=>a.asset_id.localeCompare(b.asset_id));\nstatus.pending=(status.pending||[]).filter(p=>!generatedIds.has(p.asset_id));\nstatus.failed=status.failed||[];\nfor(const item of queue.items){\n  if(generatedIds.has(item.asset_id)) item.status=\"generated\";\n}\nfs.writeFileSync(statusPath, JSON.stringify(status,null,2)+\"\\n\");\nfs.writeFileSync(queuePath, JSON.stringify(queue,null,2)+\"\\n\");\n'",
  "workdir": "/Users/lyston/PycharmProjects/video-agent",
  "yield_time_ms": 1000,
  "max_output_tokens": 6000
}
```

Do not rely on Markdown as the only place where a generated image path exists. Every image path shown in Markdown must also appear in the corresponding JSON sidecar queue/status/manifest so other skills can discover it mechanically.

### Step 5 — Generate reusable asset reference images

If running in production image mode, generate assets before storyboard frames.

Generation order:

```text
characters / turnarounds -> scenes/backgrounds -> props/UI/FX -> style references
```

For each generated asset:

1. Use `generation_prompt` + `negative_prompt`.
2. Save immediately to:
   ```text
   assets/generated/{version}/{asset_id}_{asset_key}.png
   ```
3. Ensure the same session-relative path already exists in `assets/generated/assets_generation_queue_v{version}.json.items[*].target_path`.
4. Verify with PIL or equivalent: exists, nonzero bytes, opens, dimensions available.
5. Update `assets/generated/assets_generation_status_v{version}.json.generated[]` with `asset_id`, `asset_key`, `path`, `size`, `bytes`, and `qc_status`.
6. Mark manifest asset status as `generated` only after file verification.
7. Do not mark `approved` until asset-review/human QC passes.

### Step 6 — Plan the full storyboard image set

For every storyboard shot, create a frame plan using:

```text
script context + storyboard shot row + extracted assets + generated asset images + continuity diagnosis + shot function + previous/next shot logic
```

For each shot, define:

- shot function: what this shot must communicate
- audience takeaway: what viewers should understand in 3 seconds
- previous-to-current logic: what changed from the previous shot
- current-to-next logic: what this shot sets up
- required visual cues
- likely misread to avoid
- which asset images anchor identity, scene, prop, and style
- needed variants: A only, A+B, A+B+C, or more

Write queue/status/manifest sidecars under:

```text
assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frame_queue_v{N}.json
assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frame_status_v{N}.json
assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frames_manifest_v{N}.json
assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frames_manifest_v{N}.md
```

The queue and manifest must include session-relative frame paths and, when available, session-relative `asset_image_paths` from `assets/generated/assets_generation_status_v{N}.json`. If an asset image is pending, keep the asset key in the frame plan and omit the nonexistent image path rather than inventing one.


```

```

3. Verify the file opens and update both:

```text
assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frame_status_v{version}.json
assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frames_manifest_v{version}.json
```

Downstream video generators must be able to resolve all generated storyboard stills from `storyboard_frames_manifest_v{version}.json` without scanning directories.

Default requirements:

- Real cinematic/documentary/commercial frame, not SaaS/vector/PPT/diagram art.
- Realistic lighting, camera, lens, composition, and production design.
- Clear action readability and story function.
- If the project is Chinese-market, visible people should be Chinese/East Asian unless the user asks otherwise.
- For protagonist stories, preserve character identity across all relevant frames.
- For corporate/brand films, preserve semantic continuity and business meaning rather than forcing same-person continuity.
- For phones/UI/actions, show natural user-facing use; do not stage phones unnaturally toward the camera unless the shot calls for a direct insert.
- Avoid fake logos, unreadable text dependency, malformed hands, distorted faces, extra limbs, random Western casting in Chinese-market scenes, and stock-photo posing.

If one shot contains a chain of actions or an emotional/abstract transformation, generate B/C frames to show before/after or start/mid/end states.

### Step 8 — File verification and visual QC

For every generated asset image and storyboard frame:

1. Verify file exists under the current session `assets/` directory.
2. Verify file opens with PIL or equivalent.
3. Verify dimensions and byte size.
4. Create contact sheets when useful.
5. Perform full-size visual QC before marking anything accepted.

Storyboard-frame QC must check both sequence and individual frame quality:

- Does the full sequence progress logically from first shot to final shot?
- Does each shot add new information instead of repeating the same idea?
- Does each frame express the intended action, emotion, business meaning, or transformation?
- Are characters consistent where required?
- Are hands, faces, phones, props, UI cues, and body anatomy believable?
- Is the image realistic and cinematic rather than vector/diagram/stock-photo?
- Does the casting/context fit the intended market?

Write QC/status notes in:

```text
assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frame_status_v{N}.json
assets/storyboard_frames/storyboard_frames_manifest_v{N}.md

```
